**Purpose and scope:** The project's Audit Findings Register (Document 10, section 7): every issue that a Phase 1 topic's AUDIT finds (Document 04, section 5.4; Document 08, section 6, step 7), with its agreed treatment for the new system or its OPEN status, and the topic's UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items. Claude maintains it and ChatGPT reviews it; Rama decides each finding's treatment, or leaves it OPEN (Document 05, section 11.1). It is updated with each relevant topic package, and whenever a finding is corrected, superseded or otherwise changes (update timing 5). An issue whose treatment is left OPEN, wholly or in part, also stays visible in the Open Issues Register (Document 04, section 5.5).

## How entries are recorded

- **Topic:** each topic has its own section, and every row of a section belongs to that topic.
- **Finding ID:** the AUDIT's own item ID, so that every row can be traced to its record (the A01 AUDIT's section 1.7 explains its IDs).
- **Component:** the main file of the code path; the AUDIT record lists every path, with its function or class and lines, at the study baseline `970aabf` (D-21).
- **Area and flow:** taken from the AUDIT record, shortened where marked "(…)".
- **Finding:** one plain-English line, as put to Rama in the treatment round; the AUDIT record states the finding in full.
- **Issue category:** the kinds of issue of Document 04, section 5.3, assigned by Claude when this register was created (INTERPRETATION: the AUDIT does not assign them).
- **Intended behaviour and its source; code path; actual behaviour and effective value; evidence:** one column each. Each cell gives the opening of that field in the AUDIT's own words, shortened at a clause boundary where marked "(…)", and the line of the AUDIT where the full field is recorded, under the same field name ("Intended behaviour and source", "Actual code path", "Actual behaviour and effective value", "Evidence"); where a field continues in a table, the table's lines are given too. Line numbers are those of `A01_AUDIT_2026-11-05.md` as last changed at commit `2b27af4` (SHA-256 7b5d33471bb6d837634830a4e68d4226a3ce49e6ae94f04e66c921a34af25dfc). Words such as "above", "below" or "the cited lines" refer to that AUDIT record, and line references written "L123" are to the legacy code, as in the AUDIT. Every AUDIT record cited has all four fields; the AUDIT record governs. The column "AUDIT record" names the record by its section and ID. One opening is not given word for word: C29's evidence begins with a `git grep` pattern whose vertical bar would break this table, so its two alternatives are written with "or"; the AUDIT line gives the exact command.
- **Classification:** the result (ISSUE, or UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) and the classification labels that the AUDIT record uses; KNOWN DEFECT as listed in the AUDIT's section 1.8. The AUDIT record governs.
- **Severity or impact:** not established for any A01 finding (the AUDIT assigns none). The AUDIT's own qualifiers (minor, latent, low practical risk, documentation only, a design concern), and its statements that occurrence in production is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, are shown.
- **Raised with Rama:** the date, with the Open Issues Register entry (A01-nn) or the treatment round's part or group.
- **Agreed treatment (Phase 2 input), or OPEN:** input to the Phase 2 proposal only (Document 04, section 5.5). It is not a V2 design decision, it changes no legacy finding, and no legacy behaviour becomes a V2 requirement automatically. Each group's treatment is set out once, under "A01 treatment decisions", with Rama's choice and the qualification he adopted.
- **Status:** the labels of Document 05, section 3: AGREED, or OPEN QUESTION; "AGREED in part; OPEN QUESTION in part" names the open part and the Open Issues Register entry that tracks it. UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items have no treatment. No entry is CLOSED.
- **Not entered:** MATCH items (they stay in the AUDIT, section 6.3); items that the AUDIT rejected (for A01, C21b) or handed off whole (C38b); items merged into another and counted there (AUDIT section 1.8; for example M-10 in C19); and the parts handed to other topics (AUDIT section 8), which their owning topics record.
- **Sensitive values:** none. Credentials, tokens and account identifiers are named by type only.

## A01 — System orchestration, startup, runtime and shutdown

**Source:** `A01_AUDIT_2026-11-05.md` (DRAFT; committed at `8ce3215` and last changed at `2b27af4`; passed by ChatGPT's review of 05-Nov-2026, Document 04, section 4, step 7), its sections 1.8, 6.1, 6.2, 8 and 10, with its evidence file `evidence/A01_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-05.md`, both in `doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/TOPIC_WISE_DOCUMENTATION/A01_SYSTEM_ORCHESTRATION_STARTUP_RUNTIME_AND_SHUTDOWN/`; and the Open Issues Register, entries A01-01 to A01-24. "Section" below, without a file name, means a section of that AUDIT.

**How the treatments were decided:** the 24 entries of the AUDIT's section 10(b) were discussed with Rama on 05-Nov-2026 and 06-Nov-2026 and are recorded in the Open Issues Register. On 06-Nov-2026 Claude's treatment-round file put to Rama the eight of those entries still OPEN for treatment (Part 1), the 106 findings of section 10(a) in ten groups, each with a recommended treatment (Part 2), and the UNKNOWN items and cross-topic handoffs, for information (Part 3). ChatGPT reviewed the file independently the same day and recommended a choice for each entry and group (its section 8), with the qualification behind each (its sections 3 and 4). Rama adopted those choices, with the full qualifications, as his decisions (his answers of 06-Nov-2026: "Adopt section 8" and "Include sections 3-4"). The discussion itself is not kept (Document 07, section 3); the words that decide each treatment are quoted below.

### A01 treatment decisions

Each group: Claude's recommendation as put to Rama (PROPOSED until his choice); his choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8; and the qualification he adopted with it, in the words of ChatGPT's section 4. Where a choice is B, the qualification states the exception, and it governs the recommendation where the two differ.

- **C1 (Part 1):** decided with the Open Issues Register entry A01-01 (AGREED, 06-Nov-2026: one headless start path, which an operator can invoke by hand for recovery; no separate interactive mode). The entry quotes the decision in full.
- **Group A — Start-up failures that end with the wrong exit code or message (10 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "one written start-up failure contract: every known failure has its own check, message and outcome (none ends in the generic crash code 2); each case has a test; the restart and alert rule as you agreed in A01-02 and A01-04 (3 attempts in total, then stop, Telegram and email); every required credential is named in the configuration and checked together at start, without exposing its value." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "B — retain generic crash outcome for unexpected failures; distinguish permanent from retryable failures." Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND B: accept, except clarify that the generic crash outcome remains available for genuinely unexpected/unhandled failures. Every anticipated, identified failure should have an explicit check, message, outcome, alert and test. Keep the already agreed retry/stop/alert policy from A01-02 and A01-04. Distinguish retryable transient failures from permanent configuration or credential failures; do not repeatedly retry a permanent failure without purpose. Required credential names may be checked, but credential values must never be exposed." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026).
- **Group B — Start-up order, and cleaning up after a failed start (10 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "every start-up step is guarded; any failure runs ONE clean-up path that stops what was started, closes resources and alerts; stop signals are handled from the first moment; “ready” and order flags are set only on success; an optional feature is either fully on or fully off." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "A" (accept the recommendation). Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND A, with implementation-neutral safeguards. Guard each startup step; install stop-signal handling before starting workers; use one idempotent cleanup path for partially started resources; close resources; alert; and set readiness/order flags only on success. Define critical and optional workers separately. Cleanup must not conflict with the separately governed emergency close-out path in A01-11." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026).
- **Group C — Start type, session record and configuration-change detection (11 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "besides the agreed points, the kill state is read and enforced whatever the session records say; every configuration change (added, changed or removed) is recorded and alerted once, whichever way the start ends; each start type has its own written procedure; status and dry-run as agreed in A01-05, showing what their specification lists." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "B — dry-run remains strictly read-only under A01-05." Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND B: accept the recommendation except for its treatment of --dry-run. The kill state must be read and enforced regardless of what the session record says. Start types need explicit procedures. Added/changed/removed configuration must be detected and reported. IMPORTANT CONFLICT: A01-05 already AGREED that --status and --dry-run are read-only. The group recommendation says every configuration change is “recorded and alerted once, whichever way the start ends”, which would cause writes/alerts during dry-run. Preserve the agreed read-only contract: dry-run may display detected differences in its report, but must not persist state, emit side-effecting alerts, create broker connections or otherwise mutate the system. Persist/alert changes only during a real start, with a defined once-only rule. Do not silently override A01-05." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026).
- **Group D — Time, calendar, holidays and fixed clock times (15 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "one IST time source and one market calendar (holidays, special sessions, session times), used by every part of the system; calendar data checked when loaded, with one failure rule (with A01-09); no VM-local dates, no hard-coded times or paths (with A01-13); behaviour across midnight defined." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "B — high-level consolidation accepted; exact A01-09/A01-15/A01-20 behaviour remains OPEN." Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND B: accept the high-level consolidation principle, except leave the exact calendar failure policy, NTP thresholds/reachability behaviour and VM-independent IST implementation OPEN for Phase 2 under A01-09, A01-15 and A01-20. Respect already AGREED decisions, including the no-Muhurat-trading treatment and A01-13's fixed-time/alert input. A single time/calendar authority is a design direction, not a completed specification. Define midnight-crossing behaviour and validate calendar data explicitly." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026); the parts the qualification leaves OPEN stay OPEN QUESTION (Open Issues Register, A01-09, A01-15 and A01-20).
- **Group E — Shutdown, supervision and logging at exit (11 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "shutdown is bounded within the service's stop timeout and runs in a written order; every worker can be told to stop and is waited for; logs are flushed at exit; every operator message named in a procedure exists; a heartbeat and supervision cover every critical worker (kill behaviour as decided for A01-14)." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "B — accept supervision/shutdown direction; reconcile 30-second timeout against configured 84/99-second waits and runtime unknowns." Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND B: accept the direction, except do not assume the 30-second service timeout is already a proven safe shutdown budget. The audit establishes configured wait totals of 84 seconds, or 99 seconds with flatten, against the declared 30-second stop timeout; actual runtime behaviour is not fully established. Phase 2 must reconcile the stop budget, bounded waits, escalation, remaining positions and process termination. Require explicit stop methods and joins for owned workers, supervised critical workers, heartbeat/liveness, server failure visibility and log flushing. Keep emergency close-out independent from ordinary shutdown." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026); for C25 and W-04, the kill behaviour is A01-14's, whose soft-kill and hard-kill details stay OPEN.
- **Group F — Start-up checks and their descriptions (11 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "one written list of start-up checks, each with its purpose, order, severity and outcome; checks change nothing; each is tested; the list in the documents matches the code." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "B — pure checks read-only; side-effecting actions separately named and controlled." Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND B: accept the written inventory/order/severity/outcome/test/documentation requirements, except refine “checks change nothing”. Pure validation checks should be read-only and side-effect-free. Any required initialization, repair, state transition or protective action must be a separately named and controlled startup action, not hidden inside a check. This distinction matters because the audit identifies side effects in the startup-checks module. Keep A01-15's NTP thresholds OPEN." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026); the NTP thresholds stay OPEN with A01-15.
- **Group G — Single instance and ports (2 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "with A01-16, every port the system needs is set in config.yaml and checked at start, with a clear message and alert if it is taken." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "A" (accept the recommendation). Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND A. Put required port settings in configuration, validate configuration, and check every required listener with clear failure reporting. Also require the actual bind/listen operation to handle a port-taken race: a pre-check alone cannot guarantee the port remains free between check and bind. Do not assume the instance-lock port protects a separate webhook/health port." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026).
- **Group H — Configuration, credentials and accounts (11 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "the account items are covered by A01-03 and A01-24 (one configured Zerodha client, no accounts.csv; the legacy account registry is not carried forward). For the rest: every setting in config.yaml is either read and honoured or rejected when loaded (no setting silently ignored or overridden in code); credentials are loaded once at start, never on import; size and dead-code rules are kept." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "B — scope to A01-owned configuration/startup; preserve already agreed account decisions." Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND B: accept the agreed account treatment and the principle that settings must not be silently ignored or overridden, but scope this treatment to A01-owned startup/configuration behaviour and refer other components to their owning topics. A01-03/A01-24 already settle the single configured client/no accounts.csv direction; do not reopen that. Load credentials at controlled startup, not import time; validate required configured names without exposing values. Any setting either has a documented consumer/effect or is rejected clearly." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026).
- **Group I — Internal events and error types (20 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "events and error types are defined once, each with a real sender and receiver; an event-delivery failure is reported; an unknown value (for example a product type) is rejected, never quietly defaulted; lists such as the close-out product list exist in one place; error severity drives alert routing; no dead types. The cross-topic parts (M-08, M-11, M-30) are decided in A04, A09 and A13." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "B — cross-topic architecture deferred to owning topics." Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND B: accept the quality principles, but defer central event/error architecture and cross-topic details to their owners. Define real event producers/consumers, report delivery failures, avoid silent fallbacks for unknown values, centralize genuinely shared lists, and route severity appropriately. Do not settle M-08, M-11 or M-30 in A01; their owning topics must decide the handed-off portions. “No dead types” should be a review/maintenance rule, not a reason to delete types before their cross-topic use is established." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026); the handed-off parts of M-08, M-11 and M-30 are decided by their owning topics.
- **Group J — Documentation and operator text (4 findings).** Recommendation (Claude): "in the new system, code descriptions, comments, help text and operator documents are kept in line with the code and checked at review; legacy text is not carried over." Rama's choice, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "A" (accept the recommendation). Qualification (ChatGPT's review, section 4), adopted as part of the decision: "RECOMMEND A. New-system comments, help text, operator procedures and specifications should describe the approved behaviour and be checked for consistency. Do not carry forward legacy text merely because it exists. This is a documentation-quality treatment, not a decision to reproduce any legacy interface." Status: AGREED (06-Nov-2026).
- **C32 (an ISSUE in neither list of section 10):** Rama's decision, in the words of ChatGPT's section 8: "C32: Keep as recorded; no new treatment line." Its treatment is OPEN; a treatment would need an explicit scope change. Its runtime part stays UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 6.2).
- **The UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items (Part 3):** listed for information; no treatment is decided. They stay UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED until runtime evidence is found (Document 06, section 4.2); B-41 is also the subject of A01-20.

## A02 — Configuration and effective settings

**Source:** `A02_AUDIT_2026-11-08.md` (DRAFT; last changed at `2a96068`; passed by ChatGPT's complete package review of 08-Nov-2026, Document 04, section 4, step 7), its sections 1.8, 5.3 to 9.3, 9.8, 10 and 12, with its evidence file `evidence/A02_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-06.md`, both in `doc/02_PHASE1_LEGACY_STUDY_AND_AUDIT/TOPIC_WISE_DOCUMENTATION/A02_CONFIGURATION_AND_EFFECTIVE_SETTINGS/`. "AUDIT line N" in a cell is the line of that AUDIT, as committed with this register, where the field is recorded in full.

**Entries:** 111: the 103 ISSUEs (two of them KNOWN DEFECTs, provisional, as Rama's treatments keep them (batch T3, group G1; batch T4, group G1): T-01 and F-20) and the 8 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items (L-18, V-14, V-15, V-16, T-21, F-15, F-25, F-26). Not entered: the 149 MATCH items (V-12 among them); F-02 and F-05, merged into T-02 and T-19 and counted there; F-18 (INFORMATION) and F-19 (WITHDRAWN), which are not counted (AUDIT section 1.6); part K's coverage rows; the pointer rows to A01 items; and the parts handed to other topics (AUDIT section 10), which their owners record.

**How this section differs from the A01 section:** the issue category is the AUDIT record's own (the A02 AUDIT assigns it); the severity or impact cell gives the opening of the record's own field. Every finding is raised with Rama at A02's issue discussion after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 4, step 8): the 10 findings that need Rama's intent or decision are entered in the Open Issues Register as A02-01 to A02-05 and A02-08 to A02-12 (A02-01 holds three subtopics, of which two are answered by evidence and one is put to Rama, and A02-08 holds two questions: 11 questions for Rama in all), raised on 08-Nov-2026; every ISSUE is put to Rama in the treatment round below (09-Nov-2026); and the STUDY's questions 4, 5, 7, 8 and 10, which the AUDIT kept as OPEN QUESTIONs without needing them to classify (X-01, K-15, K-01, L-01, V-01), are entered as A02-13 to A02-17, raised on 09-Nov-2026 (batch 3). Until Rama decides, an ISSUE's treatment is OPEN and its status OPEN QUESTION; an UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED item has no treatment and that status (Document 05, sections 3 and 11.1).

**How the treatments are decided:** the 10 findings with an intent question were discussed with Rama on 08-Nov-2026 and are recorded in the Open Issues Register (A02-01 to A02-05 and A02-08 to A02-12). The treatment round puts the 103 ISSUEs to Rama in five batches by area: T1, the loader (L); T2, the validator, the auditor and the snapshot (V); T3, the strategy files (T); T4, the files, the environment and the revert script (F); T5, the settings and their consumers (K, with X-01). Each batch groups the findings, each group with Claude's recommendation (PROPOSED until Rama chooses); ChatGPT reviews the batch and recommends a choice with its qualifications; Rama chooses. A treatment is UNDER DISCUSSION until Rama confirms its read-back, then AGREED. The UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED items receive no treatment. The discussion itself is not kept (Document 07, section 3); the words that decide each treatment are quoted below.

### A02 treatment decisions

Each group: Claude's recommendation as put to Rama (PROPOSED until his choice); his choice and the qualifications he adopted, in the words that decide them. Batch T1: Rama's decision of 09-Nov-2026 ("RAMA_TO_CLAUDE_A02_T1_LOADER_TREATMENT_DECISIONS", prepared in the ChatGPT chat after Rama accepted ChatGPT's recommendations "as it is"); he confirmed its read-back on 09-Nov-2026, with qualification 2 of group G1 corrected. Batch T2: ChatGPT's review of 09-Nov-2026 ("CHATGPT_REVIEW_A02_T1_REGISTER_AND_T2", section 2), whose suggested choices and qualifications Rama adopted in the Claude chat on 09-Nov-2026 ("Adopt all as suggested"); he confirmed its read-back on 09-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed"). Batch T3: ChatGPT's review of 09-Nov-2026 ("CHATGPT_REVIEW_A02_T2_READBACK_AND_T3", sections 3 and 4), whose suggested choices and qualifications Rama adopted in the Claude chat on 09-Nov-2026 ("Adopt all as suggested"); he confirmed its read-back on 09-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed"). Batch T4: ChatGPT's review of 09-Nov-2026 ("CHATGPT_REVIEW_A02_T1_T2_T3_T4", section 3), whose suggested choices and qualifications Rama adopted in the Claude chat on 09-Nov-2026 ("Adopt all as suggested"); he confirmed its read-back on 09-Nov-2026 ("Part A confirmed"). Batch T5: ChatGPT's review of 09-Nov-2026 ("A02_T4_T5_CHATGPT_REVIEW", section 2), whose suggested choices and qualifications Rama adopted in the Claude chat on 09-Nov-2026 ("Adopt all as suggested"); he confirmed its read-back on 09-Nov-2026, with group G1's owner topics corrected. Every treatment is a Phase 2 input, not a V2 design decision.

- **Batch T1, group G1 — Dates and times when the configuration is loaded (5 findings: L-01, L-02, L-03, L-06, L-17).** Recommendation (Claude): "One IST time source and one market calendar for every part of the system (as agreed in A01 group D); the next year's holidays checked well in advance, with an alert (the rule for when they are missing is the one still OPEN under A01-09); every timing check uses the configured values, never a number fixed in code; times and dates checked strictly (exact HH:MM, exact date format), with a message that names the setting; special sessions checked in full when loaded; no fallback default times (with your A02-02 input)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS", with these qualifications: "1. The exact missing-next-year-calendar failure policy remains OPEN under A01-09 for Phase 2. Detect and report a missing calendar early, but do not decide here whether the system fails or continues. 2. Preserve A01-18 as agreed on 05-Nov-2026. 3. Do not decide here whether every full normal weekend session should be traded or skipped. That separate question remains as recorded under A01. 4. Validate special-session data separately from deciding whether trading is permitted on a date. 5. Use the common IST time/calendar authority and configured values consistently. Do not invent a new timing schedule." Qualification 2 is as corrected at the read-back of 09-Nov-2026: Rama's first wording repeated A01-18's superseded first wording, and he chose "Preserve A01-18 as agreed on 05-Nov-2026" (A01-18: no trading in any Muhurat session, on any day, with no override; a full normal special session is not excluded by that rule). Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T1, group G2 — Required settings and code defaults (2 findings: L-04, L-13).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every setting comes from the configuration file. A missing setting is a load error that names the file and the key. No defaults in code; any documented value lives in the file itself" (as Rama's A02-02 input). Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT", recorded as: "1. Every setting must come from configuration files. 2. No hardcoding, code defaults or settings embedded in .py files. 3. A missing required setting must fail clearly, identifying the configuration file and key. 4. Any intended default belongs in the configuration file, not silently in code. 5. Do not change or delete existing values as part of this treatment. The full required-key inventory and migration/validation method belong to Phase 2 design. 6. The 289 legacy code defaults are an audit finding, not a ready-made list of new-system defaults." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T1, group G3 — Strict validation (6 findings: L-09, L-10, L-11, L-12, L-14, L-15).** Recommendation (Claude): "Checking at load is strict: duplicate keys are refused; no silent type conversion; not-a-number and infinity are refused; every rule written in a comment or decision is either enforced when loaded or deleted; lookup-table keys are checked against what they refer to. Required credential names are checked when loaded, and the variables checked as present at start, without ever showing a value (with A01 group A)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS", with these qualifications: "1. Reject duplicate YAML keys; do not silently accept the last value. 2. Use strict type validation; no silent coercion. 3. Reject NaN and positive/negative infinity wherever finite numeric values are required. 4. Validate mapping keys and identifiers against the entities they refer to; do not merely warn where invalid keys could cause settings to be ignored or misapplied. 5. Validate credential environment-variable NAMES as non-empty and valid. Check required variables at the appropriate startup boundary without exposing values. 6. Enforce current authoritative requirements, not every historical note, example or stale comment. Correct or remove non-authoritative text rather than turning it into a requirement. 7. Define strictness by field type and documented contract; do not reject explicitly permitted YAML representations. 8. Distinguish required from optional integrations/features. Do not make an optional service credential a mandatory startup dependency unless the feature is enabled and required. 9. Errors may identify the setting/path but must never expose secret values." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T1, group G4 — Configuration-loading failure handling (2 findings: L-07, L-08).** Recommendation (Claude): "One written failure rule for loading configuration: every failure (missing, unreadable, malformed, invalid value) gives a named error with the file and the key, and a defined outcome and alert; this sits with the start-up failure contract agreed in A01 group A." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS", with these qualifications: "1. Establish one written failure contract for missing, unreadable, malformed, invalid and internally failing configuration loads. 2. Identify the file and key/field where applicable; where no key exists, identify the file and failing operation/context. 3. Preserve A01 Group A's startup failure contract, including the distinction between permanent configuration failures and retryable/transient failures. 4. Do not repeatedly retry permanent configuration errors as though they were transient. 5. Keep operator-facing messages actionable and safe; diagnostic detail/tracebacks must be controlled and must not expose secrets." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T1, group G5 — Configuration structure and descriptions (2 findings: L-05, L-16).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every configuration file is loaded and checked in one place, not by scattered readers with their own defaults (with your A02-02 and A02-08 (b) inputs); the module size rules agreed in A01 group H are kept; descriptions match the code (A01 group J)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS", with these qualifications: "1. Establish one authoritative configuration-loading and validation boundary. 2. All configuration files in scope must be accounted for and validated; no independent bypass reader may silently introduce defaults or skip validation. 3. One authoritative boundary does not mean one enormous Python file or monolithic schema. Modular schema/model components and well-separated readers are allowed behind that boundary. 4. Apply the module-size and boundary rules already agreed in A01. Do not use the 2,516-line legacy loader as a target or carry its structure forward. 5. Module descriptions, comments and operator documentation must accurately describe the actual contract and be checked for consistency. 6. Keep strategy configuration within the same governed configuration contract, respecting topic boundaries and the Phase 2 design process." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Cross-references kept by batch T1** (Rama's decision of 09-Nov-2026): A02-02 (configuration-file authority); A02-05 (config.yaml time is the master); A02-08 (b) (broad configuration audit); A01 group A (the start-up failure contract); A01 group D (one IST time source and one market calendar, with the exact calendar failure policy, NTP behaviour and VM-independent implementation OPEN where already recorded); A01-18 (no Muhurat trading); A01 groups H and J (module size and boundaries; accurate descriptions). No OPEN A01 or A02 matter is resolved by batch T1.
- **L-18 (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED):** no treatment; whether the later commit and the 03-Sep MIS values were ever in force cannot be established from surviving runtime evidence.
- **Batch T2, V-08 (what the auditor does not inspect):** decided with the Open Issues Register entry A02-08 (AGREED in part, 08-Nov-2026: the Phase 2 input as recorded, namely validation covering every enabled trading mode, delivery included; a warning before a stop-loss percentage above 10% is accepted; the broadest practicable configuration audit, hardcoded values included; the design details OPEN QUESTION in part). Rama confirmed on 09-Nov-2026 that V-08 is decided with A02-08 and not reopened in batch T2.
- **Batch T2, group G1 — The key-usage validator (3 findings: V-01, V-02, V-03).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every setting in the configuration files is either used by the code or refused when loaded (as A01 group H and your G2 and G5 above). If a usage check is kept, it is fed by the real reads, covers every configuration file and stops or alerts as written; its description matches the code." Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT", with the qualification he adopted: "The intended outcome is sound: configuration settings must not silently remain unused, and the validator's description must match its real operation. At Phase 2, define the complete inventory and usage mechanism. Distinguish: a setting required by an enabled feature, which must have a valid consumer; a setting for a disabled/optional feature, which may be validly dormant under an explicit contract; an unknown, misspelled or unsupported setting, which must be rejected or reported according to the agreed strict contract. Do not let an incomplete usage checker claim full coverage. If retained, it must be fed by actual code reads, cover all in-scope configuration structures (including lists and all configuration files), and report accurately. Its exact implementation is a Phase 2 design matter." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T2, group G2 — The configuration auditor (5 findings: V-04, V-05, V-06, V-07, V-09).** Recommendation (Claude): "One written list of the auditor's rules, each with its purpose, severity and outcome; the list and the documents match the code (as A01 group F). A rule that cannot run is reported as 'not run' with the reason, never as PASS or silence; a start-up gate whose check fails stops the start, as fail-fast requires. Every documented check is given the inputs it needs. Timing rules measure against the real schedule (the MIS square-off passes), using the configured times (with your A02-05 input). Coverage as your A02-08 input." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS", with these qualifications: "1. Maintain one authoritative written inventory of audit rules, purpose, severity, inputs, execution point and outcome. Documentation and code must agree. 2. A check that did not run must be reported as NOT RUN with its reason. It must never be reported as PASS or silently swallowed. 3. If a required safety/start-up gate cannot complete its check, fail closed and prevent the relevant start from proceeding. This does not mean every advisory or optional audit error must stop the whole system; severity and outcome must be explicit for each rule. 4. Every check must receive the data it requires. Missing inputs must be visible as NOT RUN or a defined failure, never a false pass. 5. Correct the timing basis against the agreed configured schedule, including the actual MIS square-off passes. Preserve A02-05: config.yaml is the master time source. Exact schedule and implementation remain Phase 2 design work. 6. Keep the A02-08 coverage decision intact. Do not narrow the audit to only the currently enabled mode if the agreed scope requires all enabled trading modes." With the reason given: "'a check cannot run' and 'a check ran and found a failure' are different conditions, and advisory checks must not be confused with mandatory safety gates." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T2, group G3 — The daily configuration snapshot (2 findings: V-10, V-11).** Recommendation (Claude): "The daily record of the configuration in force covers every file that affects trading, strategy files included; it is kept so that the configuration actually loaded is always the one shown; it never stores a secret value (variable names only); details are for the Phase 2 design." Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT", with the qualifications he adopted: "Accept the principle that the snapshot must represent the complete configuration actually loaded and affecting trading, including strategy files and other in-scope files, not just the eight main files. At Phase 2, specify: 1. The authoritative inventory of files and effective inputs that affect trading. 2. How a snapshot identifies the exact configuration state used by each process/session/start. 3. How repeated states and A→B→A changes are represented without suppressing the fact that a later start loaded A again. 4. How the snapshot remains auditable and cannot be mistaken for a different configuration state. 5. Secret values must never be stored. Where environment-backed settings are involved, retain only safe identifiers/metadata such as variable names, not secret values. Do not choose the database schema, fingerprint algorithm, retention policy or implementation in Phase 1; those belong to Phase 2 design." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T2, group G4 — What the snapshot code says it logs (1 finding: V-13).** Recommendation (Claude): "Descriptions match what the code actually logs (A01 group J); what may appear in logs is set once in a written logging rule, at the Phase 2 design." Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT", with the qualification he adopted: "Descriptions must accurately match what the code logs. The permitted logging fields, treatment of account identifiers and other potentially sensitive operational metadata, and redaction rules must be established in one written logging contract during Phase 2. Do not infer that every field currently logged is automatically approved for future logging." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **V-12 (MATCH), V-14, V-15 and V-16 (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED):** no treatment; V-14's Open Issues Register entry A02-09 keeps Rama's Phase 2 input.
- **Batch T3, T-08 to T-11 (decided with the Open Issues Register):** each is decided with its entry (AGREED in part, 08-Nov-2026: the Phase 2 input as recorded; the design details OPEN QUESTION in part), with the precise scope Rama adopted on 09-Nov-2026 (ChatGPT's review, section 3). T-08 with A02-02: "Configuration files are the source of truth. No hardcoding, code defaults or settings embedded in .py files. Treat this as a Phase 2 input. It does not approve a particular schema or implementation now." T-09 with A02-03: "The existing legacy SL/TGT mechanism is scrapped. New stop-loss and target rules are to be decided per strategy after the new strategies and filters are settled. IMPORTANT: this does NOT decide that ATR remains the intended method." T-10 with A02-04: "The existing legacy strategies, filters, targets and stop-losses are scrapped for the new system. The new strategy/SL/TGT rules are to be discussed during new-system design. IMPORTANT: Rama did not choose between the legacy gap_go values 1.0% and 1.2%. Neither value is approved for V2." T-11 with A02-05: "config.yaml is the master time source; strategy.yaml is supplementary. Where applicable, the earlier cutoff governs. This applies to the morning start as well; the exact combination/edge rules remain for Phase 2." Their history stays OPEN.
- **Batch T3, group G1 — Loading strategy files (5 findings: T-01, T-03, T-04, T-05, T-16).** Recommendation (Claude): "Strategy files go through the one governed configuration boundary (T1 G5, qualification 6), under the T1 G4 failure contract: every failure, encoding errors and odd keys included, gives the named error and the alert. A missing or empty strategy folder is a named failure with the same alert. Strategy names are unique and match their file names; a duplicate is refused. The strategy configuration is loaded once per start, and that one loaded set is used everywhere: start-up checks, trading and end of day. Descriptions match the code (A01 group J)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS". Accepted: "A single governed strategy-configuration boundary and a consistent failure contract. Invalid encoding and non-string YAML keys must produce controlled, named validation failures rather than raw exceptions. Missing/empty strategy directory must be a defined failure with an appropriate operator alert. Strategy names must be unique and match their file stems; duplicates must be refused. Error descriptions must match actual behaviour." Qualifications: "1. Keep T-01's KNOWN DEFECT classification provisional as already required. Do not promote it to final without the required detailed review. 2. Do not silently expand the A02 scope to settle all boot-load orchestration. T-05 explicitly overlaps A01 C11 and A12. The Phase 2 input should require a consistent, immutable strategy configuration state for the relevant process/session and prevent checks from validating one state while trading or EOD uses another. The precise ownership and loading lifecycle must be reconciled with A01 C11 and A12, not decided by A02 alone. 3. 'Loaded once per start' is acceptable as a proposed consistency principle, but do not prescribe a single global object or implementation in Phase 1. 4. T-16 needs an explicit error-message contract: distinguish safe operator-facing messages from controlled diagnostic detail. Do not automatically expose local filesystem paths merely because the legacy code does so; equally, do not carry forward a 'path-free' claim unless the new behaviour actually satisfies it. 5. The missing-folder alert should follow the agreed startup failure and alert contracts; do not invent a new alert channel or retry policy here." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T3, group G2 — Value rules and units (3 findings: T-06, T-07, T-12).** Recommendation (Claude): "One unit convention for every percentage, stated in the file (with T1 G3). Every field's range is enforced when loaded. The schema accepts only the methods the system actually implements. Stop-loss and target methods per strategy type, as your A02-03 input (the old mechanism is scrapped)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS". Accepted: "the principle that values must be validated at load and that a schema must not accept a method that has no supported consumer." Qualifications: "1. Do not apply one blanket range rule to every percentage field. T-06 records a conflict between S3 defaults and S4's literal >0 and <1 rule. The new system needs an explicit, field-by-field contract for permitted range, zero/disabled meaning, and applicable method/feature. 2. Use one canonical numeric convention for percentage quantities where practicable (decimal fraction, e.g. 0.003 for 0.3%), but document the denominator/meaning of each field. A percentage of stop distance, target distance, price, or another base is not interchangeable merely because each field ends in _pct. 3. The legacy values 0.3 and 60/80/40 illustrate different scales/semantics. Do not mechanically convert them without defining the field contract and all consumers. 4. The schema should accept only methods approved for the new system and supported by the corresponding consumer. This is not a decision that the legacy ATR implementation must be retained or that ATR is approved for V2. A02-03 explicitly scraps the old SL/TGT mechanism and leaves new per-strategy rules for later design. 5. Define ranges and units in Phase 2 after strategy behaviour is established. Do not carry forward undocumented assumptions from the legacy schema." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T3, group G3 — Unread fields, side readers and cross-checks (4 findings: T-02, T-14, T-15, T-20).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every strategy field has a consumer or is refused (as T2 G1, with optional settings allowed to lie dormant under an explicit contract). The scanner-to-strategy map is checked against the loaded strategies at start, and duplicates are refused. No reader outside the one boundary (T1 G5). No copies of weights or thresholds in code: one source each (your A02-02 input)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS". Accepted: "Every required setting must have a real consumer; unsupported settings must be rejected. Optional/disabled-feature settings may be dormant only under an explicit contract, consistent with T2 G1. Scanner-to-strategy references must be checked before trading starts. Configuration consumers must use validated, governed configuration rather than independently parsing raw YAML with private defaults. Values such as weights and thresholds must have one authoritative source." Qualifications: "1. Make the startup cross-check explicit: every scanner-map strategy reference must resolve to exactly one loaded strategy. Also reject duplicate scanner keys and duplicate strategy names; these are distinct validation cases. 2. A dashboard, report or other separate process may need configuration data, but it must obtain it through the governed loading/validation boundary or an approved read-only representation of that validated state, not bypass the schema with its own YAML parsing/defaults. 3. T-14 crosses into A06, A07, A10 and A11. Keep each consumer-side conclusion with its owning topic. A02 should record the unused-field facts and the Phase 2 requirement, not decide those other modules' implementation. 4. T-20's single-source rule must cover signal-age thresholds as well as weights and tier thresholds. The AUDIT records 30/60-second code constants against the 90-second P9a/file statement. Reconcile ownership with A07, A15 and A18; do not let this batch silently decide their detailed runtime behaviour. 5. 'Every field has a consumer or is refused' must retain the explicit exception for optional/disabled features agreed in T2 G1." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T3, group G4 — Out-of-date descriptions (4 findings: T-13, T-17, T-18, T-19).** Recommendation (Claude): "Descriptions, file headers and the strategy guide match the code. Old text is not carried over (A01 group J)." Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATION". Accepted: "the principle that documentation, file headers and descriptions must accurately state the actual contract, and stale claims must not be carried into the new system." Qualification: "This is not permission to edit, repair or revive the legacy system or rewrite its historical locked-decision records. For Phase 2, document the evidence and carry forward only approved intent. In particular, T-17's stale historical S11 import list should be recorded as a legacy documentation mismatch; do not rewrite the legacy decision record as part of this treatment." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **T-21 (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED):** no treatment; the strategy configuration in force in production is not established. T-01's KNOWN DEFECT classification stays provisional.
- **Batch T4, F-22 (decided with the Open Issues Register):** decided with A02-01 (AGREED in part, 08-Nov-2026: Rama's Phase 2 input, no paper mode, no TEMP settings, no revert procedure and no files for them; the design details OPEN QUESTION in part). The intended daily loss limit (0.03) and `gap_fade_long.min_score` (0) are answered by evidence; the history of the revert procedure, A02-01 (b), stays OPEN.
- **Batch T4, group G1 — The TEMP revert tool (4 findings: F-20, F-21, F-23, F-24).** Recommendation (Claude): "The new system has no TEMP values, no revert tool and no files for them (your A02-01 input). Any change to a live setting goes through the governed configuration path, with change detection and alert (your A02-09 input) and the configuration snapshot (T2 G3)." Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT", as qualified: "This is a confirmation of your A02-01 Phase 2 input: no paper mode, no TEMP settings, no revert procedure and no files for them. It does not authorise editing or deleting anything in the dead legacy system. Keep F-20's KNOWN DEFECT classification provisional. Do not turn the proposed governed-change path, alerting or snapshot detail into a finished V2 design now; those remain Phase 2 design work and must reconcile with the already agreed A02-09 and T2 G3 inputs." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T4, group G2 — Configuration files outside the loader and their headers (9 findings: F-01, F-03, F-04, F-06, F-07, F-08, F-09, F-11, F-12).** Recommendation (Claude): "One written inventory of every configuration file (required or optional, owner, readers), used by every check, instead of hand-kept lists (with T1 G5). Every file is validated through the one boundary (T1 G5). One configuration location, applied to every reader. Headers and comments state the real readers and effects, and never refer to mechanisms that do not exist (A01 group J). The security monitor's own settings stay with A17 for the readers' side." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS". Accepted: "One authoritative inventory of all in-scope configuration files, including required/optional status, owner and readers. All in-scope files are accounted for and validated through the governed configuration boundary; no bypass readers with private defaults. A single authoritative configuration location/context is applied consistently to all relevant readers. Headers, comments and operator documentation accurately describe actual readers and effects. Security-monitor-specific readers and their detailed ownership remain with A17." Qualifications: "1. Do not interpret 'one configuration location' as a decision on CLI, directory layout or deployment implementation. Phase 2 must define the exact configuration-root contract and prove every reader/process uses the same selected context. No partial `--config` behaviour. 2. The inventory must distinguish files loaded by the main process from files consumed by separate processes, scheduled jobs, dashboards or security-monitor components. Each must have a declared owner and validated access path; do not force unrelated processes into an unsafe shared runtime object. 3. F-03's disputed link status remains UNKNOWN unless evidence resolves it. The treatment must not label the links placeholders or real without proof. 4. F-07's security.yaml reader findings must be handed to A17 for the consumer-side investigation; A02 records the configuration governance requirement, not A17's implementation. 5. No legacy file, header or code is to be edited as a result of this treatment. Corrections/documentation apply only to approved new-system design records in Phase 2." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T4, group G3 — Environment variable names (5 findings: F-10, F-13, F-14, F-16, F-17).** Recommendation (Claude): "One declared list of the environment variable names each process and feature needs, derived from the configuration, checked at the right start-up point without showing any value, an optional feature's names required only when it is on (T1 G3, qualifications 5 and 8). The template lists every name the code reads. Secrets come only from environment variables, never as plaintext in a configuration file. Account items as already agreed in A01-03 and A01-24 (one configured Zerodha client, no accounts.csv)." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS". Accepted: "One declared inventory of required environment-variable NAMES by process and feature. Validate names and check presence at the correct startup boundary, without ever displaying or recording secret values. Optional-feature variables are required only when that feature is enabled and required. The example environment template accounts for all required names, including scheduled-job requirements. Secrets are supplied through environment variables, never plaintext configuration values in Live. Account configuration follows the already agreed A01-03 and A01-24 decisions; do not create a second account source." Qualifications: "1. Keep the distinction between a variable name and its secret value absolute. Logs, validator output, diagnostics, snapshots and reports must never reveal values. 2. Do not require every optional integration's variable at global startup. The feature/process boundary and the point at which the variable becomes mandatory must be explicit. 3. The inventory must be complete across the main process and scheduled/separate processes, but it should be derived from declared ownership/configuration rather than a second hand-maintained list that can drift. 4. F-15 remains UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED and is not resolved by accepting G3. F-26 also remains UNKNOWN; do not infer actual deployed environments. 5. The one-client/no-accounts.csv statement is a cross-reference to A01-03 and A01-24, not a new account-design decision in A02." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **F-15, F-25 and F-26 (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED), F-18 (INFORMATION) and F-19 (WITHDRAWN):** no treatment; F-02 and F-05 are counted under T-02 and T-19. F-20's KNOWN DEFECT classification stays provisional.
- **Batch T5, K-03, K-07 (the stop-loss offset part) and K-34 (decided with the Open Issues Register):** confirmations of Rama's existing intent answers, not fresh design decisions. K-03 decided with A02-10 (AGREED in part, 08-Nov-2026: Rama's Phase 2 input; the design details OPEN QUESTION in part): "Record the intent as detecting fills fast enough to protect positions, within broker limits. Do not choose 2 seconds or 15 seconds here; the interval remains a Phase 2 design detail." K-07 recorded in two separate parts: "(a) Stop-loss offset: decided with A02-11. The same configured offset applies to normal and crash-recovery stops; no hidden constant. (b) Emergency exit buffer: remains in G2 and receives the G2 treatment below. Do not merge this with the stop-loss-offset decision." (A02-11: AGREED in part, 08-Nov-2026.) K-34 decided with A02-12 (AGREED in part, 08-Nov-2026): "Estimated slippage/costs and actual slippage/costs remain separate. Do not collapse estimates and realised values into one field or measure."
- **Batch T5, group G1 — Settings that govern nothing (12 findings: K-01, K-08, K-09, K-13, K-14, K-18, K-25, K-26, K-28, K-29, K-30, K-32).** Recommendation (Claude): "Every setting either has a working consumer or is refused when loaded. A setting for a feature that is off may lie dormant only under an explicit contract (T2 G1). A safety setting that says 'on' must really act, or the system must show that it is off. Examples: the liquidity check, the trailing master switch, feed reconnects, broker back-off. The consumer-side design stays with each owner topic (A04, A05, A06, A09, A10, A11, A12, A13, A14, A18 and others)." Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT CLAUDE'S PROPOSAL". Accepted: "Every setting must either have a real working consumer or be refused when loaded. A setting for a feature that is off may remain dormant only under an explicit contract, consistent with T2 G1. A safety setting represented as ON must actually operate; otherwise the system must clearly establish that it is off. Consumer-side behaviour and detailed ownership remain with the relevant owner topics (including A04, A05, A06, A09, A10, A11, A12, A13, A14, A18 and others as applicable)." Qualifications: "1. This is a Phase 2 input, not approval of any particular consumer implementation or default. 2. Do not use a dormant setting as an implicit fallback. The off/dormant contract must be explicit and validated. 3. Do not broaden A02 into deciding the design of liquidity checks, trailing targets, feed reconnection, broker rate-limit handling or other consumer behaviour. Those remain with their owner topics. 4. A configured safety control must never appear enabled while the actual consumer silently ignores it." Owner topics corrected by Claude in the read-back and confirmed by Rama on 09-Nov-2026: the recommendation and the adopted text first named A07, which owns none of these 12 findings; each finding's owners are named in its AUDIT record. Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T5, group G2 — Configured values bypassed by fixed numbers or copies (12 findings: K-02, K-06, K-07 (the emergency exit buffer part), K-10, K-11, K-15, K-19, K-20, K-23, K-27, K-31, K-33).** Recommendation (Claude), with Rama's A02-02 input and T3 G3: "One source per value: no fixed copies or constants of configured values in code, dashboards, pre-flight or reports. Every consumer reads the governed configuration (T1 G5). One alert configuration used by every notifier; one e-mail definition; one sentinel location; product codes from configuration. A failure never silently weakens a configured safety setting such as fail_closed." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS". Accepted: "One authoritative source per configured value. No fixed copies or constants in code, dashboards, pre-flight checks or reports where those are meant to consume the configured value. Consumers use the governed configuration boundary. One alert configuration is applied consistently across notifier call sites; e-mail definitions, sentinel location and broker product-code mappings do not drift into parallel copies. A failure must not silently weaken a configured safety rule, including fail_closed." Qualifications: "1. 'One source per value' does not mean one giant configuration file, one global runtime object, or one shared process context. Phase 2 must define ownership and access boundaries appropriate to each process and domain. 2. The authoritative value and its unit/meaning must be explicit. A dashboard or report may format a value for display, but must not maintain a competing operational copy or reinterpret its meaning. 3. K-07's emergency exit buffer is distinct from the stop-loss offset already decided with A02-11. Record both parts separately; the G2 treatment applies only to the emergency-buffer part. 4. K-02's trading-hours source must reconcile with A02-05 and the relevant A01 time/calendar decisions. This treatment does not select a new trading schedule or resolve edge rules. 5. K-31's fail-closed behaviour must remain fail-closed when the required source cannot be loaded or validated. No fallback may silently turn the control off. 6. K-19's SMTP settings must have one unambiguous semantic contract for transport/security mode. Do not assume that a boolean with the same name means the same transport mode across libraries. 7. Keep the detailed consumer-side implementation with the owner topics. This is a governance input, not a design freeze." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T5, group G3 — Settings read in different ways (8 findings: K-04, K-05, K-12, K-16, K-17, K-21, K-22, K-24).** Recommendation (Claude): "Each setting has one meaning and one reading rule: the same test, unit, default (none, per A02-02) and failure rule wherever it is read. No program silently raises or replaces a loaded value. Out-of-range values are refused when loaded (T1 G3). Key names follow the field contract for units (T3 G2). Reading a key that does not exist is an error." Rama's choice: "B — ACCEPT WITH QUALIFICATIONS". Accepted: "Each setting has one defined meaning, unit, type, permitted range, default policy and failure rule. All consumers apply that semantic contract consistently. No program silently raises, replaces or reinterprets a loaded value. Out-of-range values are refused under the already agreed T1 G3 validation principle. Unit-bearing names and values follow the field contract agreed in T3 G2. Reading a nonexistent key is an error, not an UNKNOWN display state or silent fallback." Qualifications: "1. 'Same reading rule' means the same semantic contract and effective value. A consumer may use a typed adapter or presentation conversion, but it must not change the underlying meaning, unit, default or failure policy. 2. Do not silently convert fractions to percentages or vice versa. The declared unit must be unambiguous at the configuration boundary and preserved through calculations and displays. 3. No new code defaults are authorised; preserve T1 G2 and A02-02. Any allowed default must be explicit in governed configuration and must not be invented by a consumer. 4. A missing or misspelled key must fail at the appropriate governed validation boundary with a named error; a dashboard must not quietly report UNKNOWN for a key that the contract requires. 5. Do not use this A02 treatment to decide downstream business logic or consumer ownership. Those remain with the relevant owner topics." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
- **Batch T5, group G4 — A stale comment (1 finding: X-01).** Recommendation (Claude): "Comments state current premises (A01 group J). How the receiver is bound and authenticated is decided by its owner topic (A06) in Phase 2." Rama's choice: "A — ACCEPT CLAUDE'S PROPOSAL". Accepted: "Comments must state current premises, consistent with A01 group J. Receiver binding and authentication behaviour remain for owner topic A06 to establish in Phase 2." Qualification: "This treatment corrects the governance expectation for documentation only. It does not decide the receiver's bind address, authentication mechanism or security design." Status: AGREED (09-Nov-2026).
